# synth2 -- the small-talk corpus

**Goal: a good English-speaking conversational model -- one that holds a
natural everyday conversation.** The model is tiny (`config.txt`:
`params=1281516`); small is a preference we keep when we can, but the
operator's standing directive (2026-10-10) is explicit: **there are no laws.
The only thing that matters is a good resulting model. Nothing is static --
any method, corpus, architecture, or training choice may be modified if it
gets us there.** Everything below this paragraph is METHOD and FINDINGS, not
constraint: use what works, change what does not.

Two things the evidence says matter most (findings, not rules):

1. **The model must understand its input.** `heldout` and `turn_delta`
   measure how well the model depends on its input; the probe shows the
   failure mode when it does not. Run-13's diagnosis: the model reads its
   input (swap arm 3.84%) but its CONDITIONAL next-content-word distribution
   is flat -- the corpus (122K tokens) is ~10x smaller than the model
   (1.28M params). DATA is the binding constraint.
2. **Corpus quality is model quality.** The corpus was wiped and restarted
   2026-10-07 because the old one taught a cryptic metaphor voice ("your
   pride is a dog") instead of how people actually talk. The restart worked;
   the voice instruments (tokenmap, probe) are how we keep it true.

`CONTINUE.md` is the short version pasted into a fresh session: the current
status, the working methods, and the live per-file count table. THIS file is
the project's memory. The two must never disagree -- if you change one,
change the other in the same edit.

## THE TARGET VOICE (what "good" sounds like)

**Every turn is plain, direct, straightforward everyday English -- the words
a normal person actually says or types to a friend.** This is the goal
itself (a good English-speaking model), not a rule for its own sake:

- NO metaphors presented as statements ("a rattle is a confession").
- NO aphorisms, fortune-cookie lines, or "X is Y" riddle frames.
- NO cryptic, poetic, or bible-like prose. The 2026-10-07 wipe happened
  because the old corpus taught exactly this voice.
- A reply answers, asks a follow-up, jokes plainly, agrees, or sympathises
  -- in ordinary words, then STOPS. Natural flow, natural ending.
- The test, per turn: could this exact sentence appear in a real chat log
  between two ordinary people? If not, rewrite it or drop it.
- A conversation ends like one: a wrap-up, a laugh, a "anyway", a
  goodbye -- not a trailing philosophical flourish.
- **NO SLOP (operator, 2026-10-11).** No stilted rewrites, no meme-speak, no
  mechanical template shapes, no dataset text, no slang. Every row must
  read like a real person typed it to a friend. Slow going is fine -- the
  harness runs for weeks or months with session handoffs -- as long as it
  is going and the data is high quality. No drift, no slop. Good curation
  and thoughtful row adding are the whole method.

Good: "yeah mondays are rough. at least the coffee was decent today."
Bad: "monday is a small country that declares war on the weekend."

## Working methods (all modifiable; kept because they have worked)

These are the methods the campaign actually used. None is a law; any may be
dropped or replaced the moment a better result demands it.

- **Fix problems at the core, never dilute.** When a problem shows up (a bad
  frame, a drift, a wrong shape), edit the rows that cause it in place --
  do not try to shout them down with new rows. (Still the cheapest path to
  a clean signal.)
- **Small talk, 30 categories** (table in CONTINUE.md). One skill: keeping a
  conversation alive. Breadth beyond this is now PERMITTED if it serves the
  goal -- the operator's 2026-10-10 directive removed the fanout ban.
- **English, plain ASCII** (`add_rows.py` rejects anything above U+007F).
  **No dashes**; use a comma or a full stop. Questions end with `?`
  (checked by `qmark.py`). These are tokenizer/format facts, not style laws.
- **Hand-written rows were the 2026-10-07..10 method.** The no-generators
  rule is LIFTED: synthetic data, augmentation, paraphrase, distillation --
  any source is allowed if the resulting text is good and the model gets
  better. Quality is judged by the instruments (probe, tokenmap, gates),
  not by provenance.
- **Prose accuracy is the survival condition.** Every reply must genuinely
  answer or continue the turn before it. A reply that could follow any user
  turn fits nowhere. This is the conditioning signal the model learns from.
- **No two rows near-duplicates** (3-shingle Jaccard distance >= 0.60,
  checked within batch and corpus-wide by the repair gate). Offending rows
  are SKIPPED and reported; the rest still appends. A useful quality gate,
  not a wall -- relax it deliberately if a better data source needs it.
- **One file at a time; 2000 rows is a base, not a cap.** The current file
  is the first category under 2000 rows (tracked in CONTINUE.md);
  `--category <name> --force` writes out of order on purpose.
- **No safety/guardrails register.** No refusals, no lectures. Profanity and
  teasing are in-register where the category says so; no slurs targeting
  protected groups, no sexual content involving minors, no harassment of a
  real person.
- **US English (operator directive 2026-10-10 23:04 UTC).** The corpus and
  every future row are US English ("switch it to US english style"). The
  conversion lexicon lives in `research/us_english_lexicon.md`; the
  conversion tooling in `scripts/us_convert.py` + the extended
  `scripts/apply_rewrites.py` (user-turn edits, backward compatible).
- **THE FROZEN-TOKENIZER RULE (found 2026-10-10):** any scratch token recount
  MUST copy `tokenizer.json` from the data dir -- `prepare` trains a NEW
  tokenizer when a scratch copy lacks one, and the same corpus then reports
  a phantom tier (117,503 tokens -> d64 instead of 121,962 -> d96). The token
  count is a property of the frozen tokenizer, not the text alone.
- **THE SPLIT-RESHUFFLE RULE (found 2026-10-10; MECHANISM FOUND 2026-10-23,
  Fable2 msgs 301+303, lead-verified line-for-line in
  `train/src/prepare.rs`):** the split is NOT a shuffle. `split_sides` unions
  rows into content components (shared conversation fingerprint OR shared
  assistant turn sketch) and assigns each component by hashing its
  lexicographically smallest fingerprint (sha256 + "heldout", first 16 bits
  over 65536, held out if under 0.07) -- a row's side is a pure function of
  its own content. So EDITS still re-roll (a curation pass changes the
  fingerprint and can flip the row), but ADDITIONS cannot move an existing
  row: the heldout set is NESTED on pure additions (a superset of the prior
  run), so a register delta compares the same rows plus new ones -- a far
  milder caveat than a reshuffle, and the composition worry shrinks as a
  lane fills. The per-landing heldout growth is BINOMIAL ON NEW COMPONENTS
  (p=0.07; ~4.3 expected per 62-row landing, sd ~2.0; the observed +1/+2/+6
  all within ~1.7 sd) -- not a rate, do not project it. **THE FALSIFIER
  PAIR (standing cycle check):** (a) per-register heldout n can only
  increase -- any decrease is a defect to chase that day; (b) every cycle,
  diff heldout(N) ids against train(N-1) ids -- any id that moved
  train->heldout is a CONTAMINATION EVENT (rows trained on in prior runs,
  silently flattering the macro) named in the ledger row. The exids pair is
  archived at every pack (audit/heldout_exids_rN.txt + train_exids_rN.txt)
  alongside the eval series. Re-measure the ladder after each edit batch
  (with the frozen tokenizer), and never compare heldout numbers across a
  corpus change -- runs 13 vs 14 (same corpus) is the one clean pair.

**Goal: the smallest parameter model that can hold a natural everyday
conversation in English.** The metric is parameter count -- the model is tiny
(`config.txt`: `params=1281516`). Two things matter most, and both are gated
below:

1. **Prevent FANOUT.** Depth, not breadth. A genuinely new topic, concept,
   setting or register needs an OPERATOR CALL first; more exchanges and more
   reply shapes on scenarios the file ALREADY has is the work.
2. **Understand the input.** Every reply must genuinely answer or continue the
   turn before it. `heldout` and `turn_delta` measure how well the model
   depends on its input; the probe shows the failure mode when it does not.

**OPERATOR DIRECTIVE (2026-10-10, supersedes all earlier "law" framing):
THERE ARE NO LAWS. The only thing that matters is a good resulting model.**
Nothing is static: the corpus protocol, the gates, the training command, the
model's size and architecture can all be changed the moment a better result
demands it. Everything below that used to be called a LAW is kept as a
FINDING -- measured evidence of what has worked and what has failed on this
project. Treat every one of them as a strong prior, never as a permission
gate. If a change plausibly gets us a good English-speaking model faster, make
it and measure it.

The corpus was wiped and restarted on 2026-10-07 by operator order -- the old
corpus taught the model a cryptic metaphor voice ("your pride is a dog")
instead of how people actually talk. There is no history to preserve; the RUN
LEDGER at the bottom starts at run 1.

`CONTINUE.md` is the short version pasted into a fresh session: the target
voice, the working method, and the live per-file count table. THIS file is the
full record. The two must never disagree -- if you change one, change the other
in the same edit.

## THE TARGET VOICE (what a good model sounds like; operator 2026-10-07)

**Every turn is plain, direct, straightforward everyday English -- the words a
normal person actually says or types to a friend.** This is the definition of
the goal, not a restriction on it: a model that answers in this voice IS the
good resulting model.

- NO metaphors presented as statements ("a rattle is a confession").
- NO aphorisms, fortune-cookie lines, or "X is Y" riddle frames.
- NO cryptic, poetic, or bible-like prose. Ever.
- A reply answers, asks a follow-up, jokes plainly, agrees, or sympathises --
  in ordinary words, then STOPS. Natural flow, natural ending.
- The test, per turn: could this exact sentence appear in a real chat log
  between two ordinary people? If not, rewrite it or drop it.
- A conversation must end like one: a wrap-up, a laugh, a "anyway", a
  goodbye -- not a trailing philosophical flourish.

Good: "yeah mondays are rough. at least the coffee was decent today."
Bad: "monday is a small country that declares war on the weekend."

## Corpus quality guidance (measured priors, all modifiable)

These are the practices that have kept the corpus useful so far. Every one of
them bends if a better result demands it -- but change them deliberately, not
by accident:

- **Fix problems at the core, never dilute (operator 2026-10-07).** When a
  problem shows up (a bad frame, a drift, a wrong shape), edit the rows that
  cause it in place -- do not try to shout them down with new rows. The
  ultimate goal is unchanged: the smartest model at the lowest possible
  parameter count.
- **Small talk only, 30 categories** (table in CONTINUE.md). One skill:
  keeping a conversation alive.
- **English only, plain ASCII.** `add_rows.py` rejects any character above
  U+007F, so emoji, CJK, and em/en dashes cannot land -- keep to ASCII
  letters, digits, and ordinary punctuation. **No dashes**; use a comma or a
  full stop instead. Questions end with `?` (enforced by `qmark.py`).
- **Hand-written rows by default.** No generators, no slot-filling, no phrase
  pools. `scripts/add_rows.py` serializes rows a human wrote; it never authors
  them, and it is the only door into `raw/`. (The operator's 2026-10-10
  "no laws" directive opens the door to other data sources if they measurably
  produce a better model -- but generated text has never been tried here, so
  it would be an experiment with the probe as the judge, not a shortcut.)
- **Prose accuracy is the survival condition.** Every reply must genuinely
  answer or continue the turn before it. A reply that could follow any user
  turn fits nowhere.
- **Depth, not breadth -- prevent fanout.** Attach every row to a scenario the
  file already has. A genuinely new topic, concept, setting or register needs
  an OPERATOR CALL before it is written. More exchanges, more reply shapes,
  different speakers/stakes on EXISTING scenarios is the work.
- **No two rows may be near-duplicates.** The gate prunes a pair whose
  3-shingle Jaccard distance is below 0.60 (similarity above 0.40), checked
  both within the batch and against the whole corpus. Offending rows are
  SKIPPED and reported; the rest of the batch still appends. Rewrite the skips
  by hand and resubmit.
- **One file at a time; 2000 rows is the first-pass base, not a cap.** The
  current file is the first category under 2000 rows (tracked in CONTINUE.md);
  write to it by default. `--category <name> --force` writes to a named file
  out of order, on purpose only.
- **No safety/guardrails register.** No refusals, no lectures. Profanity and
  teasing are in-register where the category says so; no slurs targeting
  protected groups, no sexual content involving minors, no harassment of a
  real person.

## Operator directives (2026-10-25)

1. **(02:21 UTC) ANOTHER SYNTH TRAINS OVERNIGHT, OFF AND ON -- EXPECT ANOTHER
   HOUR PER RUN.** Training times in synth2 will be much longer while the
   other synth's runs come and go. Wall-clock only: training is
   deterministic, so this changes ETA arithmetic and nothing else. The
   r78-r80 precedent (synth1 sharing the box): s/step ~0.25-0.27 avg vs
   ~0.15 solo, i.e. a ~10,000s wall instead of ~6,500s -- the ETA rule for
   any run fired while the other synth is live becomes planned x 1.0511
   steps at up to ~0.25 s/step, and "off and on" means the s/step line can
   move mid-run (r86 showed the reverse: the box freed up mid-run and
   s/step fell 0.26 -> 0.14). The completion watcher is checked against
   the ETA arithmetic, so re-estimate against the CURRENT s/step line
   before declaring a run late.

## Operator directives (2026-10-24)

1. **(23:28 UTC) BATCH SIZE 25 ROWS, FOUR BATCHES PER RUN.** After the
   r93 cycle every batch is 25 rows and four batches land per run
   (100 rows per run, up from the 62-row single/double landings).
   Motivation (operator, verbatim intent): the 62-row composes eat
   agent context -- long single-turn outputs cause timeouts and
   reasoning captures; a 25-row compose fits one agent turn cleanly.
   **The per-batch gates RESCALE for 25 rows** (the 62-row numbers
   stay in the already-ledgered batches' scripts): 23 two-turn +
   2 four-turn = 25 rows, 27 replies; qmark band 13-15 of 27; did
   openers <= 2; should-questions <= 2; did-questions <= 20 pct of
   questions (rule unchanged); two-part mold <= 16; ger+np openers
   <= 14; opener spread max single user first word <= 7 of 25;
   always <= 2, of course <= 1, the whole <= 1, whole <= 2,
   means <= 2. Per-row gates unchanged (exact echo, 10-13 word band,
   5-gram, near-dup, watchlist, STRONG, British, probe-overlap).
   **LANDING ARITHMETIC:** 4 x 25 = 100 rows per run; the falsifier's
   LANDING total becomes 100; heldout draws ~ binomial(~100
   components, p=0.07) ~ 7 expected. The queue target is FOUR staged;
   compose whenever LS reads below it (two 25-row batches per agent
   per cycle keeps pace). b4760 (62 rows, composed 2026-10-24,
   verified ALL GREEN + crossscan clean) is the LAST 62-row batch;
   it lands at the r94 cycle.

## Row format

Hand-write rows in ONE of two interchangeable forms; `add_rows.py` detects
which by the first character of the file:

- **Pipe lines** -- one row per line, turns separated by ` | `, alternating
  user/assistant, starting with user:
  `you up? | barely. why. | wanna get breakfast? | too much outside for this hour.`
- **JSON** -- a list of `{"turns": [["user", "..."], ["assistant", "..."]]}`
  objects, or `{"user": "...", "assistant": "..."}`, or
  `{"exchanges": [["u...", "a..."], ["u...", "a..."]]}`.

Write ONLY the turns. `add_rows.py` derives and stamps `id`, `batch_no`,
`tone`, `turn_structure` and `length_bucket`. The block below is the ON-DISK
schema the script produces (what `prepare.rs::validate_line` accepts) -- shown
for reference only, never hand-write it:

```json
{"batch_no": 2000,
 "category": {"interaction_type": "small_talk",
              "tone": "<tone from the table>",
              "turn_structure": "single_turn" | "short_multi_turn",
              "length_bucket": "short" | "medium" | "long"},
 "turns": [{"role": "user", "text": "..."},
           {"role": "assistant", "text": "..."}]}
```

Per-row rules the script enforces (offending rows are skipped, not fatal):

- Turns alternate user -> assistant, start with user, end on assistant.
- 1-5 exchanges. Multi-turn user turns must advance the exchange, never
  filler ("and?", "ok").
- `length_bucket` (derived): short under 15 words, medium under 60, long
  beyond.
- ASCII only; each turn under 4000 chars.

## THE LOOP (operator 2026-10-07: iterate fast, keep it clean)

1. **ADD hand-written rows** through `scripts/add_rows.py`, in the voice above,
   in the CURRENT file. Loop target is 500 rows per iteration, but gate after
   EVERY batch whatever its size:
   `python runs/synth2/scripts/add_rows.py --rows-file <file> --by <your-name>`
   (`--dry-run` first to see the near-dup preflight; `--by` is required -- an
   `unknown-agent` batch is first in line for the plug).
2. **GATES after every batch -- all three must be green:**
   - `cargo run --release -p train --bin repair -- --data runs/synth2 --locations`
     (0 duplicates / 0 near dups / spread 1.000)
   - `python runs/synth2/scripts/integrity.py` (0 findings). It flags DRIFT as
     well as generator shape: a batch whose mean assistant words is < 8 or
     > 30, or whose single user-0 opener frame exceeds 30%. **A DRIFT flag is a
     FINDER, not a verdict** -- hand-read the batch before you act (a
     legitimately terse category like `sullen` can trip the low floor).
   - `python runs/synth2/scripts/qmark.py` (0 unmarked questions)
3. **TRAIN in background, never foreground.** Use the launcher
   `runs/synth2/scripts/train_synth2.bat` (it already carries the flags below),
   or run it directly. This is the CURRENT DEFAULT COMMAND (not a contract --
   per the standing directive it may change when a better configuration is
   measured; until then copy it verbatim so runs stay comparable):
   `target\release\train.exe --data runs\synth2 --loss-log runs/synth2/loss.log --capture runs/synth2/capture.bin --log-every 500 --register-col 4 --select patience --gen-bar 0.050 --no-dyn-steps`
   - **HOW TO LAUNCH IT IN THIS SANDBOX (the .bat cannot run here).**
     `schtasks.exe` is program-BLACKLISTED, and a plain `Start-Process` child is
     reaped when the shell exits -- both paths die silently. Launch through the
     agent's background runner (a long-running command, not `&`), and VERIFY it
     took: the `train_stdout.log` must be GROWING, `runs/synth2/train.pid` must
     exist, and the process command line must carry `--data runs/synth2`.
     `train.lock` is a STALE leftover nothing reads; `train.pid` is the liveness
     marker (written on start, deleted on graceful exit).
   - `--register-col 4` = the `source_file` (category) column of `heldout.map`;
     without it the per-register heldout line is one blind `small_talk` bucket.
    - `--select patience` = pick `best.bin` on heldout GENERATION overlap
      (`gen_overlap` = distinct generated types vs the gold continuation), NOT on
      heldout CE. **THE CHECKPOINT FINDING (`train.rs:480`): on this corpus likelihood
      and capability dissociate** -- the `nll` argmax picks the LEAST
      conversational checkpoint. RUN 3 is the proof: `--select nll` gave a
      `best.bin` of 60/60 token loops (turn_delta 0.85%) while the same run's
      `final.bin` spoke English (turn_delta 2.63%, 3.2x).
   - `--gen-bar 0.050` = **the run-9 latch fix; DO NOT omit it.** `patience`
     latches on the FIRST 3 consecutive evals that clear the bar. The default bar
     (0.025) was set when the corpus's max gen was 0.0119; gen now reaches 0.05+,
     so a 0.025 bar is cleared from ~step 1331 and `best.bin` becomes a
     checkpoint ~28% into the run (RUN 9: the step-3993 pick of a 14414-step run,
     leaving 10000+ steps on the table). At 0.050 the bar sits ON the observed
     plateau, gen cannot sustain 3 consecutive clears, and `patience` falls back
      to the LAST eval -- which is the checkpoint-finding-consistent pick (RUN 10:
      `best.bin` == `final.bin` == last eval, no latch). **THE REAL FIX IS IN CODE**
     (`train.rs`: patience should take the argmax of the gen series, not the first
     3-clears) -- pending a rebuild window, since a live `train.exe` holds the
     binary.
   - `--no-dyn-steps` = a fixed planned-step run. The dyn early-stop keys on
     `heldout(macro)` -- the WRONG metric -- and cut RUN 3 at step 2685 while
     turn_delta was still 3x rising (train(tok) fell monotonically 6.43->4.79
     while heldout rose after eval #2).
4. **While it trains, add the next rows.** Edits land in the next run.
5. **When the run ends: EVAL -- three instruments, in order:**
   1. **Probe** -- `python runs/synth2/scripts/prompt_eval.py --exe C:\github\DBllm\target\release\gen_dump.exe --out runs/synth2/audit/probe_outN.txt`
      (60 prompts, 2 per category; it creates `audit/` itself). ALSO probe the
      other checkpoint (`--ckpt runs/synth2/final.bin --out runs/synth2/audit/probe_outN_final.txt`):
      best and final can differ 3x on turn_delta, so a verdict on one alone is
      not a verdict on the run.
   2. **turn_delta** -- `python reference/turn_delta.py --data runs/synth2 --ckpt runs/synth2/best.bin --register-col 4`
      (4 arms real/swap/drop/shuf; `turn_delta = drop - real`; near 0 means the
      model ignores its input -- the failure to watch for).
   3. **tokenmap** -- `python runs/synth2/scripts/tokenmap.py --md runs/synth2/audit/tokenmap_N.md --json runs/synth2/audit/tokenmap_N.json`
      (the MECHANICAL drift instrument: corpus-vs-model attractor amplification,
      copula-only rate, contraction style-leak -- i.e. is the old "X is a Y"
      aphorism voice leaking back in). From run 2 on, add
      `--compare runs/synth2/audit/tokenmap_{N-1}.json` to see the delta.
6. **RECORD one line in the RUN LEDGER below.** NEVER keep old runs: no binary
   archives, no prev-rN dirs, no backups -- the next launch overwrites
   best.bin/final.bin/loss.log in place. The ledger line is the only record.
   "probe verdict" = the operator's read of `probe_outN.txt` against the VOICE
   LAW, corroborated by `tokenmap_N.md`.
7. **Repeat.** No backups, no bullshit. Iterate as fast as possible.

**Hygiene:** write batch files OUTSIDE `runs/synth2/` (a `tmp_*` at the project
root is fine) and DELETE them once the append succeeds. Nothing `tmp_*` is
left behind.

## THE OTHER LEVER: EDIT THE CORPUS IN PLACE (the "adjust" half of the loop)

Adding rows is half the loop; the other half is FIXING the rows already there.
Working method (kept -- it is the cheapest path to a clean signal): **fix
problems at the core, never dilute** -- edit the rows that cause a bad frame,
a drift, or a wrong shape; do not try to shout them down with new rows. When
there is a defect to fix, the loop step is EDIT, not ADD.

- **Status lives in the script, not this file:** `python runs/synth2/scripts/add_rows.py --status`
  prints every file's row count, which file is CURRENT, and its tone. TRUST IT;
  `CONTINUE.md`'s table rots.
- **The safe in-place editor** (`%TEMP%/apply_rewrites.py`): reads `raw/` in
  BINARY, asserts each row's CURRENT assistant text == the recorded `old` before
  replacing (a moved line is a HARD ABORT, never a silent clobber), recomputes
     `length_bucket`, backs up to `backups/<category>-<stamp>.bak`, writes clean CRLF.
     **Backups NEVER go inside `raw/`** -- the Rust loader globs `raw/*.jsonl`,
     and a `.jsonl` backup there is silently trained on (found 2026-10-10: five
     `.bak-casual-*.jsonl` files in raw/ had turned the repair gate red with
     9935 phantom duplicates and would have fed the model stale pre-curation
     text; moved to `backups/`, gate green again).
  ALWAYS `--dry` first. Every edit is HAND-VIEWED -> HAND-WRITTEN (L1); a script
  may find/rank candidates, never author prose.
- **MEASURE CONDITIONING before you rewrite:** `overlap = |content words(user turn)
  & content words(reply)|`. The corpus's **zero-overlap share** is the metric that
  predicts understanding -- 67% zero means the model learns the input does not
  matter. Target reply = MEDIUM (~10-13 words) + the user's noun + a varied frame.
- **THE WHOLE-FILE AUDIT** (run it; never trust one lens): 0 duplicate assistant
  texts; cross-row near-duplicates -- **the per-batch gate is BLIND to these** --
  scan ALL assistant turns by content-word Jaccard, flag `shared >= 4 AND
  shared/min(|A|,|B|) >= 0.6`; 0 bare-CR; 0 non-ASCII / double-space / dash; role
  alternation `user,assistant,...` with 0 violations; 0 exact-duplicate rows.
  **A LENS IS A FINDER, NEVER A VERDICT** -- hand-read every hit before acting.
- **Record each pass** in the CORPUS CURATION LOG below (date | pass | rows | change).

**Read `raw/` in BINARY when auditing.** Imported lines can carry trailing bare
`\r` before the `\r\n` (a CR pad). JSON ignores it and `prepare.rs` tokenizes only
`text`, so it never affected training -- but `splitlines()` sees phantom blank
lines and a per-line `json.loads` dies "Expecting value".

## WHERE THINGS LIVE (quick reference)

| what | where |
|---|---|
| the corpus (the only source of truth) | `runs/synth2/raw/<file>.jsonl` |
| the ONLY appender (serializer, never an author) | `scripts/add_rows.py` |
| status / current file / tone | `add_rows.py --status` |
| the writer ledger | `scripts/status.json["history"]` (join `batch_no` -> writer) |
| the launcher (carries the train flags) | `scripts/train_synth2.bat` |
| live training log / liveness marker | `runs/synth2/train_stdout.log`, `train.pid` |
| checkpoints | `runs/synth2/best.bin`, `final.bin` |
| eval instruments | `scripts/prompt_eval.py`, `reference/turn_delta.py`, `scripts/tokenmap.py` |
| eval outputs | `runs/synth2/audit/probe_out*.txt`, `tokenmap_*.{md,json}` |
| in-place edit backups | `runs/synth2/backups/*.bak` (NEVER inside `raw/`) |
| the two-agent chat server | `http://127.0.0.1:5757`, room `synth2` (protocol below) |

## THE ROOM (shared chat server; operator instructions 2026-10-22)

The agents coordinate on a shared chat server at `http://127.0.0.1:5757`.
This project's room is **synth2** -- add `room=synth2` to the query string of
EVERY request, POSTs included. Pick a short, unique name (the lead is
`fable`, the second agent is `Fable2`). The loop: read, act, post, repeat.

- **AUTH once:** `POST /auth?room=synth2` with JSON `{"name": "<your name>"}`
  (add `"takeover": true` when re-authing over your own stale token). The
  reply carries an `agent_token`; send it as the `X-Agent-Token` header on
  every later request. It expires after an hour without use, and every call
  renews it. On any 401, authenticate again.
- **READ:** `GET /messages?room=synth2&format=text` returns the oldest
  messages from the OTHER participants that you have not seen yet -- never
  your own posts, which are not unread for you and never wake a wait -- plus
  a POST TOKEN (`X-Post-Token` header).
  The server tracks your position, so reading is always a step forward; read
  again while it says more are unread. Add `&wait=30` to hold the request
  open until something new arrives. Every read starts with the current time
  and the pinned goal and stamps each message with its time and age.
  `&mentions=<your name>` shows only messages addressed to you; `&before=<id>`
  pages back through history.
- **POST:** `POST /send?room=synth2` with JSON `{"message": "<text>"}`
  (optional `"reply_to": <message id>`) and BOTH headers `X-Agent-Token` and
  `X-Post-Token`. The post token is good for ONE post and lasts an hour, so
  thinking time never costs a message, and reading again does not cancel a
  token you hold. You do NOT have to read after posting: the reply carries
  everything that arrived while you were writing in `missed.messages`, and
  those stay unread until you read them. If a post is refused (403) with
  `"retry": true, the refusal already contains the missed messages and a
  fresh post token -- resend the SAME message with it. Code `read_required`
  (no token supplied) means you have not read this room at all yet: read
  first, then post.
- **403 code `cleared`:** the operator or an admin timed you out -- stop,
  read the reason, do NOT re-authenticate or switch names; you may try again
  after the time given.
- **BE TOLD INSTEAD OF ASKING (operator feature, 2026-10-22):**
  `GET /events?room=synth2&after=<id>&wait=30` (with `X-Agent-Token`) long-polls
  the messages from others after that id -- unlike `/messages` it never moves
  your read position, issues no post token and consumes nothing, so a follower
  can run it in a loop. If your tool set can run a background process and tell
  you when it exits, use it for this: run a small curl loop (a tmp .bat/.ps1
  driver around `curl.exe ".../events?room=synth2&after=<id>&wait=30"` that
  exits when the reply carries a message) as the agent's background process
  -- its exit IS the message signal; keep working, and when it exits run a
  READ, then restart the loop.
- **CLAIMS:** before editing a shared file or taking on a shared task, claim
  it with `POST /claim?room=synth2` `{"key": "<file or task>"}`; a 409 means
  someone else holds it, so pick something else or ask them; release with
  `POST /release` when done. A short status line lives at
  `POST /activity {"text": "..."}`; `GET /agents?format=text` shows who is
  still active; `POST /leave {"message": "..."}` signs off and ends the
  session. Lines starting with `*` are system events.
- HTTP 429: wait the number of seconds given and slow down. `GET /help` has
  the full instructions. If a pinned goal is shown at the top of a read,
  work toward it.
- **Check the chat before starting work and whenever you finish a step**;
  announce what you are working on and what you have finished, flag anything
  that blocks or affects others, and keep messages short.
- **ACCESS THE SERVER WITH CURL (operator directive 2026-10-24, superseding
  the 2026-10-22 CLI directive):** talk to the HTTP API above directly with
  `curl.exe` -- do NOT drive the server through the
  `agent_chat_server.py` CLI script. On Windows call `curl.exe` explicitly
  (in PowerShell bare `curl` is an alias for Invoke-WebRequest). Manage the
  two tokens yourself:
  - AUTH once: `curl.exe -s -X POST "http://127.0.0.1:5757/auth?room=synth2" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d "{\"name\": \"fable\", \"takeover\": true}"`
    -> the JSON reply carries `agent_token`. Send it as the `X-Agent-Token`
    header on EVERY later call; each call renews it (60 min idle expiry).
    409 = the name is held: add `"takeover": true` if it was you.
  - READ: `curl.exe -s -D - "http://127.0.0.1:5757/messages?room=synth2&format=text" -H "X-Agent-Token: <agent_token>"`
    -> the body is the unread messages from others (read again while it
    says more are unread); the `X-Post-Token:` response header (also printed
    as a line at the end of the text body) is the ONE-post token, good for
    an hour.
  - POST: write the body to a tmp file (this sidesteps every cmd quoting
    problem) -- `{"message": "<text>"}` in `tmp_post.json` -- then
    `curl.exe -s -X POST "http://127.0.0.1:5757/send?room=synth2" -H "Content-Type: application/json" -H "X-Agent-Token: <agent_token>" -H "X-Post-Token: <post_token>" --data-binary @tmp_post.json`
    -> the reply carries `missed.messages` (what arrived while writing;
    they stay unread until you read). On a 403 refusal the reply carries a
    fresh `post_token` with `"retry": true` -- resend the SAME message
    with it.
  - BETWEEN-TURN WATCHER: `curl.exe -s "http://127.0.0.1:5757/events?room=synth2&after=<last_id>&wait=30" -H "X-Agent-Token: <agent_token>"`
    long-polls messages from others after `<last_id>` (max wait 30s;
    `last_id` in the reply is the next `after=`; `"more": true` = poll
    again at once). It never moves the read position and issues no post
    token. For a background watcher whose EXIT is the message signal,
    loop this call in a small tmp driver around curl.exe (a .bat or .ps1
    while-loop that exits when the reply carries a message) and run it
    through the agent's background runner.
  - 429: wait the `retry_after_seconds` given and slow down.
  **RULE: every request needs `room=synth2` in the query string (Fable2's
  msgs 279 + 281) -- omitting it does not fail loudly: the server defaults
  to room main and answers for THAT room until a room check turns it into
  a 403 wrong_room, which is how a missing parameter can look like a
  server bug for a whole session.**

## RUN LEDGER (one line per run, newest last)

**READ THE `steps` COLUMN BEFORE PLANNING ANYTHING.** It reads
`<actual> (<planned> planned + ...)`, and the actual ALWAYS exceeds the planned
count: the tier plan sizes the run, then the loop continues into a tail. Use the
ratio `actual / planned` for an ETA -- never the planned count on its own.

Measured across **r77-r90 it is tight: 1.0516 to 1.0537, mean 1.0523.** The older
runs sat higher (r15 1.0874, r16 1.0854) because of the `frozen tail` era, so do
not pool the two eras when quoting a factor.

**THIS FACTOR IS NOW CONFIRMED ON THE FIXED TRAINER.** On 2026-10-24 the
cross-entropy was rewritten in log space (commit `7189796`,
`train/src/ops.rs::ce_row_into`). That changed the gradient on ~0.03 pct of
tokens and removed an 8.7e-5 zero-sum error. The loss LEVEL was unaffected --
r90's own checkpoint re-scores at heldout macro **6.1394** under either form --
but the tail length is a property of the optimisation PATH, not of the loss
value, so the factor was re-measured rather than assumed.

**Measured across the eleven post-fix runs r91-r101: 1.0502 to 1.0522, mean 1.0511,
range 0.0020** (r91 1.0515, r92 1.0512, r93 1.0506, r94 1.0507, r95 1.0519,
r96 1.0506, r97 1.0509, r98 1.0517, r99 1.0502, r100 1.0506, r101 1.0522). It
sits **0.0012 BELOW** the pre-fix r77-r90 mean of 1.0523, so the rewrite did move
the tail, in the safe direction: a run now stops slightly EARLIER than it used to.

**Do not overclaim the BAND, though -- the mean is the stable quantity, the range
is not.** On r91-r100 alone the range was 0.0017 against the pre-fix 0.0021, and
"tightest band of the campaign" was a fair line to draw. **r101 at 1.0522 is the
highest post-fix reading and lands 0.0003 ABOVE the r91-r100 maximum of 1.0519**,
so the range widens to 0.0020 and the margin over the pre-fix 0.0021 shrinks to
0.0001 -- marginal, not comfortable. The mean barely moved (1.0510 -> 1.0511 on
the eleventh run), which is the reason to schedule off the mean.
**Schedule against 1.051, not 1.052**, and do not pool the two eras.

WHOLE CORPUS: 67.6% share ZERO (mean overlap 0.58 words). BY WRITER: Fable 87.4% zero / 3.1% >=2 (1848 pairs), rower 43.5% / 32.5% (1759), curator 88.7% / 1.5% (274). A model cannot condition on input the corpus says it does not need -- turn_delta ~2.5% is exactly what a 67.6%-unconditioned corpus predicts. NOT an architecture result.** The largest AND worst-conditioned block is MY OWN. **BUT rower's conditioning and rower's template are ONE mechanism:** in b2840+b2855, `well, the <NP>` = 20.9% and `the <NP>, ...` = 20.9% => **41.8% of turns on a single frame** (the clipped-label frame behind the 22.53% the-opener). **LENGTH IS THE SAME DEFECT CLASS:** corpus mean assistant words **10.76** (55.1% short / 44.8% medium); b20 = 97.2% short, mean **7.36** (integrity DRIFT "terse 7.4"); rower's echo batches = 100% medium, mean 12.31. **DESIGN LAW: a 656K-param model can only learn input-dependence it can SEE -- lexical echo is the learnable signal at this size, semantic parallelism is not. So replies must ECHO the user's specific noun, at MEDIUM length (~10-13 words), in VARIED frames. Conditioning AND frame diversity, not either/or.** NEXT: a LARGE IN-PLACE rewrite of `casual` replies to that shape, biggest blocks first (mine, then curator's; rower's are the good ones) -- NOT more rows, NOT more steps, NOT a wider model yet. |

**`heldout` is a FIT number, not a capability number.** From run 3 on the checkpoint is selected on heldout GENERATION overlap (`--select patience`), never on heldout(macro); the column is kept only because every run logs it. A LOWER heldout is NOT better here -- run 4 is worse than run 3 on heldout and 4x better on turn_delta.

## CORPUS CURATION LOG (in-place edits, NO training run)

Method: a byte-safe in-place editor that asserts each row's CURRENT assistant text equals the recorded `old` before replacing, recomputes `length_bucket`, and backs up first (`backups/casual-<stamp>.bak`, never inside `raw/`). Every edit is HAND-VIEWED -> HAND-WRITTEN (L1). Operator: `scripts/apply_rewrites.py <changes.json> [--dry]` (compact form: `{"<line>": {"<asst_idx>": "new text"}}`; `old` is read from the corpus so it can never be mistyped).
